Alex didn’t believe me, said you maybe went for a bath in the creek.” She laughs slightly. They pull apart, but Kara has to avert her eyes from her adoptive mother.

“Why are you not angry?” she whispers.

Eliza smiles sadly. “Oh, I am. But more than that, I am relieved to have you back. Maybe I would have been happy for you had you written us a letter in the future telling us you are well, but since you had no plan, no food, no equipment except for a stolen pair of rubber boots, you’re better off back here.”

Kara swallows. She wipes her eyes with her sleeve, then looks back at Eliza. “I’m sorry. I truly am.” She sniffles. “How did you know I wanted to go with Lena?” she asks quietly after a moment.

“I do read the newspaper, Kara,” Eliza says and smiles. “Even if you didn’t tell me, I could put two and two together. I saw Lionel Luthor’s obituary, then an article that the Luthors were going back to the Klondike. Then Lena came here in tears, and you received a letter through her maid.” She sighs, then continues.  “Did she ask you to come?”

“No,” Kara shakes her head. Next to Eliza, Alex raises an eyebrow in disbelief. “Truly. We wished each other farewell in our letters. I didn’t see her at the harbor, there were so many people. I don’t know what I would have done had I boarded the ship.”

Eliza hums in response. Then she asks, “Did you see Charlie at the harbor? I heard him leave this morning. I know that he sometimes visits the native people by the harbor, but he left earlier than usual. But he’s a curious boy, so maybe he wanted to see the last steamer before the winter.”

Kara freezes in her spot. She had forgotten about Charlie. “I heard him leave too. I saw him walking down the street and tried to follow him, but lost him. I did not see him at the harbor, no.”

“You followed him?” Alex asks.

“Yes,” Kara says and bites her lip. “I thought it strange that he would leave so early.”

“Do you think he boarded the ship?” Alex asks, and there is worry in her voice. Charlie has only been with them a short time, but she has grown fond of the boy. Kara suddenly realizes how devastated Alex would have been if both her and Charlie had boarded the ship without a warning.

Kara shakes her head. “I don’t know.”

Alex looks like she wants to ask more, but a knocking on their front door interrupts her. A female voice calls out “It’s Sam!”. Kara almost laughs. It’s a scene that’s almost familiar by now. Alex blushes, then brushes past Kara to go into the shop and open the door.

Kara takes the time to pick up her bag from the floor and put it onto a chair. She looks at Eliza guiltily, but her adoptive mother only gives her a sad smile.

“We should eat breakfast, don’t you think?” she asks, and Kara nods.

And then Samantha Arias is standing in their kitchen, clutching Alex’ arm and crying.

“Oh my, what an eventful morning!” Eliza exclaims when she sees her. “What’s the matter with you, dear?”

Sam sobs. “My daughter ran away this morning. I cannot find her.”

The three Danvers look at her in alarm. Alex rubs Sam’s arm in an attempt to console her. “Have you gone to the police? You should report her missing!” Eliza says, but Sam shakes her head.

“I worry that she boarded the steamer that left this morning. She has been asking questions about the gold find recently, reading every newspaper article she can find. It’s my fault.” Sam says and sobs. She dabs at her eyes with a handkerchief. “I’m a terrible mother. We moved around so much when she was younger, she doesn’t know what a home is. She’s wild and adventurous and her mother’s love isn’t enough to keep her home! Oh!”

Alex’ eyes go wide at Sam’s lamentations. She pulls Sam into a hug. “That is not true” she says when she lets go. “You’re an excellent mother, and Ruby loves you. We will find her. You should ask the police for help.”

Sam shakes her head, but doesn’t reply. She sniffs, and the kitchen falls into silence for a moment. Then, Kara gasps as she comes to a realization.

“Charlie left this morning, too! He went towards the harbor. Do you think-?” she cannot finish the sentence.

The others look at her. Desperation is etched into Sam’s features. She covers her mouth with her hand as she lets out another sob. “Oh, the children wanted an adventure, they’ve grown tired of the city life! They will be so lost in the unforgiving cold of the North!”

As Sam’s sobs fill the room, they hear the backdoor open. Sam stills, and the four of them turn towards the door. They hear a huff and a thud, and then Kara cannot believe her eyes.

Ruby Arias is standing on the threshold of their kitchen, where just minutes before Kara had stood. She’s dressed in a gown that is too big for her and that she has to hold up lest it touches the floor. A fashionable hat is askew on her head, and her hair is up in a messy do. Most unusually, her face is covered in white powder, giving her tan skin a pale complexion that makes her look strange. There’s a suitcase next to her that she dropped onto the floor. She’s breathing heavily as she looks at the four women in the kitchen.

Then, she breaks out into a mischievous smile. “They’re not back yet, it seems?”

 

***

The morning of their departure is dull and chilly, and Lena’s mood is to match. She washes and lets Eve help her get dressed. Refusing the food she is offered, she makes Eve pack it into a paper bag, even though she knows she won’t want to eat it on board, either. Eve packs her suitcase while Lena sits on her bed in silence.

Lillian is fussing about, giving Lex last instructions about what to take with him and what to leave there. Lex had agreed for Eve to accompany them, and while it is a small comfort for Lena, she rather wishes she could stay behind. Spending the rest of the mourning period alone in her chamber suddenly seems much better than spending it on a ship. Benoit arrives at the house but Lena ignores him, and he seems not to care to wish her a good morning, either.

The carriage ride to the harbor is silent. Lillian clutches Lena’s hand the same way she had done at church. Neither of them speaks. Lena doesn’t look out the window, doesn’t say goodbye to the city she has come to love. There are already a few dozen people at the harbor when they arrive, but Lena pays them no mind lest she mistakes one of the faces for Kara’s.

They’re among the first to board. Lillian wishes them farewell, and Lena and Lex both curtsy in front of their mother. Benoit shakes her hand. Lillian doesn’t cry like some of the other women on the docks, instead her face is rigid like stone. Lena wonders what goes through her mind. Wonders if she’s sad to see her son leave her, if she’s happy to see her adoptive daughter leave. Or if she dreads the emptiness of the mansion, the work that was left behind for her to do. But there is no chance to ask, and before Lena knows it, they are walking across the gangplank and onto the ship.

They’re being shown towards the first-class cabins. There is only a limited number, and so Lena is told to share one with Eve. She doesn’t mind, prefers it over being alone even. However, upon entering the cabins, she is taken aback. The chamber is small, with two simple beds on each side, and a trunk in the middle. The carpet is simple, the light dim. If this is first-class, Lena thinks, she isn’t sure she wants to know where the poorer passengers will be sleeping.

Lena sits down on the bed. Lex and Benoit have gone to the cabin they are sharing next to theirs. Eve is pacing around the small space, looking at the details in the wallpaper and opening drawers. She turns around to Lena.

“Are you alright, Miss Luthor? You’ve been rather quiet this morning. Would you like to eat your breakfast now?” Her tone is as eager as ever, and Lena wonders if she ever feels melancholy at all.

Lena only shakes her head. They can hear commotion in the hallway, a sign that other passengers have boarded. Lena dreads being stuck on a ship with strangers for days on end. Somehow, it feels worse than the train ride the Luthors had taken from New York to Seattle over two years ago. There will only be water. No mountains or fields, no passengers boarding and departing in the next town.

“Your brother asked us to join him on deck to watch the departure,” Eve says. “I will go up. Will you join us?”

Lena sighs. The last thing she wants to do is wave goodbye to the city she’s come to call home, to search for a familiar face in the crowd that she’ll never get to see again. Lena wonders if Kara will be at the harbor. She doesn’t know what made her include the day and time of their departure in her letter. Maybe she had hoped for Kara to rescue her, or to kiss her good-bye in front of everyone like in one of those dime novels that Eve likes to read.

“No, Eve. I think I will rest here, I’m afraid I might become sea-sick. I will join you in the dining hall for lunch.”

Eve nods and leaves, but not without giving her another concerned look. Lena doesn’t know if Benoit will be on deck or not, but she figures that Eve must be excited to spend time with Lex. She lets out a low laugh. At least there is one person who is happy to be stuck on a ship.

Lena doesn’t know how long she sits on the bed, her suitcase unopened, staring at the wallpaper at the opposite side of the room. She listens to the people on the ship, the sounds of heavy footsteps in the hallway, the shouts of men, the cries of a baby.

After a while, she can hear two hushed voices in front of her door. Her brows furrow. She thinks she recognizes one of the voices, but cannot seem to place it. Then, there is a gentle knock on her door.

“Yes?” she asks.

The door opens. Two young servants enter the cabin, carrying fresh sheets. Lena wants to tell them that her bed is made and she doesn’t need anything when she recognizes the girl. Her face is partly obscured by a flat cap, but there’s no mistaking her.

“Ruby!?” Lena exclaims, jumping up from the bed. “What-, what are you doing here?”

Ruby shushes her with a finger on her mouth. “Shh! We’re here to help you. You’ve met Charlie?” Ruby gestures towards the boy next to her without giving Lena any other explanation.

Lena, now standing in front of the two, regards the boy. His flat cap is pulled low as well, but she thinks she vaguely remembers him - the new apprentice at the Danvers shop. He gives her a shy nod.

“How-?” Lena starts, but is too stunned to speak.

“We’ll explain later. You don’t want to go North, do you?” Ruby asks.

Lena shakes her head slowly.

“Good.” Ruby says and grins. “I don’t want you to go either. We’re here to help you go back.”

Lena’s eyes go wide. “Ruby! You must be insane! The ship leaves soon, I don’t know the time but there is not much of it left! And where will you two go? You don’t plan on going North, do you?”

Ruby rolls her eyes and starts taking off her cap and her apron. “No time for discussion, Lena. Take off your dress and switch clothes with me.”

Sam’s daughter has been strong-willed and mischievous ever since Lena met her, and it is why Lena is so fond of her. Now, however, standing in the first-class cabin of a steamer set to depart within the hour, and the possibility of Eve returning anytime, Lena is struck by the girl’s astuteness. She can only gulp and do as instructed.

Lena dresses in the maid’s clothes. The skirt is a bit tight and the sleeves too short. She doesn’t ask where Ruby has gotten them, and Ruby doesn’t say anything about them, either. She takes off her earrings and necklace and puts them in her pocket. Her own dress is a bit too large for Ruby, but the pattern suits her well.

“What are you taking with you?” Ruby asks as she puts on Lena’s hat.

Lena looks at her suitcase. It contains a few of her dresses, a winter coat, her toiletries, Jack’s and Kara’s letters and her magazines. She has another one that is being stored somewhere else on the steamer for the journey.

“Just this suitcase,” she whispers, and Ruby nods.

“Great. Hand it over!”

Lena gasps. “What?”

“Lena,” Ruby sighs. “We must be quick. You will leave with Charlie first. Then I will come out and leave the ship. I will do my best not to make a fuss, and I have an excuse for when someone approaches me. Agreed?”

“Agreed. Only because I haven’t a better idea.” Lena says and snorts.

Ruby pushes the sheets she had previously carried into Lena’s hands. “Make sure nobody sees your hands, anyone giving you a critical look will know that those hands haven’t worked a day in their life.”

Lena blushes, but does her best to grab the sheets in a way that her pale, delicate fingers are covered.

“Do you have any cosmetics?” Ruby asks.

“In the suitcase. Be careful not to spill them.” Lena instructs. Her heart is pounding, and she decides to simply play along, lest she freeze in her spot thinking about the consequences of this hasty, juvenile plan.

Ruby gives her a thankful look. “Go now. We’ll meet in one to two hours. Charlie knows the way.”

“Thank you, Ruby. Please be careful.”

Lena and Charlie leave the cabin. There are only a few people in the hallway now, Lena assumes that most will have gathered on deck by now. A clock on the wall tells her that it is 45 minutes until departure. She follows Charlie, her senses on high alert in case she spots her brother. Charlie, however, doesn’t lead her right off the ship.

They walk down a staircase, and Lena realizes they’re in the second-class deck. The hallway is impossibly narrower than upstairs, and a glance into the rooms reveals dorms of eight or ten bunk beds in one cabin. Lena shudders at the thought of having to sleep in the same room with multiple strangers for so many nights.

“Hey, you!” a man shouts after them when they’ve almost reached the end of the hallway. Lena’s heart stops for second. Charlie turns around, and Lena hesitantly follows suit.

“You about to change sheets? The mattress on my bed is moldy. Bring me some extra sheets!” The man instructs in an accent Lena has never heard.

Not used being talked to in such a manner, she almost starts to protest until she remembers the role she’s playing. She stays silent, thinking it best to let Charlie speak.

“Understood, Sir. We’ll bring them along!”

The man nods, satisfied, and disappears back into the cabin. Lena lets out a long sigh of relief.

Charlie chuckles. “It means the disguise is working.”

Lena presses her lips tightly together. All she wants is to get off the steamer and have solid ground under her feet again. They walk up another flight of stairs and down another hallway. Nobody bothers them this time. Charlie seems to know his way around, and Lena wonders if he has worked on a ship before. He seems too young to, but then again, she knows nothing about working at his age. Her experiences on ships are limited to travelling first-class with her family, over the Atlantic Ocean and back again.

Making sure no one is around, they place their sheets on a stool by a closet. Then, finally, they make their way up onto the deck. Lena is nervous, wonders if her brother might be looking their direction just this moment, but Charlie wastes no time walking towards the gangplank. Lena sees a man standing on the dock collecting tickets, and a young man making his way onto the steamer. He nods when he passes them, and Lena nods back, not meeting his eyes.

Then they’re walking down the gangplank. Lena keeps her eyes on her feet, but she can nevertheless see that the crowd is large. She feels like all eyes are on them, like at any moment someone will recognize her disguise and call out. Nothing happens. As they step down the gangplank, the man on the dock gives them a curious look.

“We were sent to collect the suitcase of a gentleman who is about to board,” Charlie explains calmly. Lena wonders if he rehearsed his sentences together with Ruby.

The man nods. “Don’t be late. I’m not waiting on anyone.”

And just like that, Lena is free. She wants to cry, but she knows she mustn’t draw attention to herself. Charlie tugs on her sleeve. “Let’s wait over there for a moment.”

They wait and observe the crowd, pretending to be waiting for someone. After a while Charlie starts walking away, and Lena follows him. They walk through the crowd, nonchalantly, Charlie keeping his eyes on a non-existing target ahead of them. A little bit away from the people, Charlie stops and takes a deep breath.

“Okay. Now we hide until the ship leaves” he whispers to Lena.

Lena’s brows furrow. “Hide? Where?”

Charlie only gestures for Lena to follow him. Away from where the big steamer is docking, there is a parcel of land between the containers and rails that Lena has never noticed before. She can see canvas tents and canoes, and she realizes with horror that people must live there.

“From here we can see the steamer, but are save from anyone looking for you” Charlie explains as if they walked into a park downtown.

They sit down on a boulder, but Lena cannot keep her eyes from wandering. There are native children looking at her with curious eyes, and she pulls the flat cap deeper into her face.

“Where does your family live, Charlie?” she asks after a while.

At first, Charlie doesn’t respond, and Lena has an apology on the tip of her tongue for asking the question, but then he sighs. “My mother is dead. The white settlers burned down the longhouse we lived in.” He fiddles with the hem of his shirt. “My father comes here in the summer with his canoe to trade. He dislikes the city.”

“I’m sorry,” Lena says for lack of anything else to say. “I know it’s not the same, but I lost my mother when I was young, too.”

Charlie only hums in response. His eyes are on the steamer, seemingly trying to make out Ruby in the distance.

“Do you enjoy living with the Danvers?” Lena asks. She is desperate to keep the conversation going. She doesn’t know Charlie, but if Kara and Alex trust him, then so does she. He seems to be Ruby’s friend, and she doesn’t know why he agreed to join Ruby’s plan, but her future is currently placed in the palm of his hand, and so she wishes to get to know him a little bit.

Charlie turns around, with a smile on his face this time. He nods. “Yes. They’re friendly. Alex teaches he me how to read and write.”

“Really?” Lena asks. “That’s exciting!”

Charlie grins. Then, however, his expression turns somber. “But she’s sad, I think, without her father. Eliza told me that he had a seat at City Hall and supported my people, until he passed away suddenly. I wish he was still there, so Alex can find work somewhere else.”

Lena says nothing. Kara tends to avoid talk about Alex and Jeremiah, and Lena had never known much about Alex, as the older sister tended to avoid Lena in turn. She swallows thickly.

They sit there in silence after that. Lena’s legs start to become stiff, but she doesn’t dare get up and move around. Around them, people come and go, entering the tents made from simple canvas planes, or arriving and leaving by canoe.

The crowd, as far as they can see them from their spot, seems to become antsy after a while. And sure enough, they can hear the engine roaring and see smoke escaping the funnels. They hear yelling and shouts of ‘Farewell!’ as they watch the steamer leave the harbor.

Lena almost expects it to turn around, expects that Lex has discovered her absence and bribed the captain, but the steamer only moves steadily towards the horizon until it disappears. Lena breathes out audibly, and Charlie looks relieved, too. They wait for a few minutes more until the crowd disperses. Lena is all too aware of the fact that Lillian might linger by the harbor still, and so she doesn’t hurry.

Charlie gets up. “Should we go?”

“Where are we going?” Lena asks.

Her back hurts from sitting on the hard boulder for so long. She only knows of one place in the city she wants to go, only one face she wants to see, and relief floods through her when Charlie laughs.

“Harbor Road, of course!”

11. Chapter 11

Notes for the Chapter:

hey everyone, i'm really sorry to have kept you waiting. the story had already been finished, but i wanted to edit the next chapters again before i posted them. but then life happened, and i had no time or energy. now i read through the story again and decided to post the remaining chapters. this one is rather long, so get comfortable :) lots of things happening and moving forward (literally).
as always, thanks for reading and for sticking around! <3

Kara can only stare at Ruby in disbelief. The others are just as stunned. The first one to move is Sam. Rushing towards her daughter, she hugs her, in the same manner that Alex had hugged Kara just minutes ago.

“Oh, look at you!” Sam cries. “Where did you go, what did you do?” She holds Ruby at arm’s length to look her up and down, then kisses her head.

Ruby submits to her mother’s affections, but laughs when she finally wiggles free. “I’m alright, mother!” She wipes some powder off her face with the back of her hand. “I boarded the steamer that left this morning, together with Charlie.”

Kara gulps. So their initial fears had been warranted, then.

“We convinced Lena to come back to Seattle.”

“Oh, Lord!” Sam cries. Kara cannot believe what she’s hearing.

“She and Charlie went together, and I followed after a while,” Ruby continues and gestures down at herself.

It then hits Kara like an ox-cart. She’s seen the dress Ruby is wearing before. It’s Lena’s. Sam seems to come to the same conclusion at the same time, and starts making a fuss.

“Oh, Ruby, what have you done? Where are Lena and Charlie now?”

Ruby shrugs. “Charlie told me he knows a place to hide. They were going to wait until the ship has departed and then come here.”

“Was this Lena’s plan?” Sam asks. She looks faint. “Did she ask you to do this?”

“No, no, mother” Ruby rushes to reassure her. “It was my plan, and Charlie agreed to help me. Lena was so unhappy about going with her family, and I didn’t want her to go either. Neither did you, or Kara.” She glances at Kara, who hasn’t moved an inch since the girl’s arrival.

Sam sends a look full of disbelief towards Alex, then walks over to sit on the nearest chair. Kara worries she might actually faint. “Ruby,” Sam starts, slowly enunciating each syllable.

“Are you aware that you kidnapped the daughter of one of the wealthiest families in America? They will notice her absence, they will find out how she left the ship, oh!” She puts her head in her hands. “They will come after you, and me, just as we’ve made a life for ourselves here!”

Ruby grimaces and looks down at her feet. It’s clear as day that she hasn’t spared a thought of the aftermath of her actions, hasn’t thought further than getting Lena off the ship. There is a child-like fear in her eyes when she looks around the room that has fallen silent.

Alex fills a glass with water and rushes towards Sam’s side. Sam nods thankfully, and Alex puts a comforting hand on her shoulder. Kara wants to say something, wants to find comforting words for both mother and daughter, and herself. But the words are stuck in her throat.

Eliza speaks up. “Why don’t we eat breakfast, and get some clarity for what we can do-“

She is interrupted by the creaking of the backdoor and hushed voices. Kara almost laughs. The scene from earlier repeats itself. The laugh gets caught in her throat, however, when she sees the two figures hesitantly entering the kitchen. Ruby squeals and claps her hands. “You made it! I knew you’d make it!”

Kara recognizes Charlie first. His clothes are unfamiliar, and he’s holding a flat cap in his hands as his eyes dart timidly around the room. Logically, Kara knows that the person next to him must be Lena, but she still has to do a double take.

The cap is pulled deep to cover her face, her hands are hidden in the pockets of her washed-out apron. When Lena looks up, their eyes meet. Kara gasps. The sight of Lena in front of her, that she thought she’d lost forever, takes the breath from her lungs at once.

The sob that escapes Lena’s lips reverberates in Kara’s chest, and in the next instant they are by each other’s side. Kara holds Lena tightly, breathing in the familiarity of her perfume like a sailor drowning at sea. Lena presses her cheek against Kara’s shoulder, her chest heaving with grief, exhaustion and relief.

Kara softly kisses the top of Lena’s head. Her cap must have fallen off, because when they finally separate, Kara can see Ruby holding it in triumph. It makes Kara laugh, and she gives Ruby an acknowledging nod.

Alex clears her throat. “Damn,” she whispers, and Kara cannot help but laugh again.

Next to her, Lena cracks a smile, too. Ruby and Charlie exchange a grin.

Sam coughs. “Lena,” she starts. “I know you came back out of your own volition, if through this unexpected help, and as Ruby’s mother I-“ she stops when Lena holds up a hand.

“You needn’t apologize, Sam,” Lena says quietly. Her voice is hoarse, and Kara realizes she must have cried. Nevertheless, the sound is music in her ears, like a long-forgotten childhood rhyme.

“Ruby and Charlie were braver than I could ever hope to be” Lena explains. “I had simply given up, submitted to my fate. Just one more thing that was out of my control in my life, I was so used to being told where to go, how to behave.”

She continues. “I’m grateful for you two, truly. Ruby, you’ll make a strong, confident woman one day, and I hope that you will be able to choose who you marry.” She smiles at Sam’s daughter, who looks uncharacteristically shy in the moment.

Then, she turns to the boy. “And Charlie, who had only met me once very briefly, but who agreed to help me regardless. You’re most bricky, and I can learn a lot from you.” Charlie blushes and looks to his feet.

“I don’t regret coming with you. However, I must say that you were lucky that you weren’t seen leaving the ship by my mother in this dress, Ruby.”

Sam’s eyes go wide. “Lillian? She’s still here?”

“She is. She decided not to go with us” Lena confirms with a nod.

Ruby gasps. “Your mother …? I didn’t … I didn’t know …”

“What are we to do?” Kara whispers and grips Lena’s arm.

A sudden panic washes over her. She imagines Lillian Luthor knocking on their door, using her family’s name and status to ruin the Danvers’ business that had only just recovered. She shudders. She has only just gotten Lena back, she cannot handle losing her again.

Judging from the stares the others send in Lena’s direction, she is not the only one thinking it. Sam breathes out audibly. Alex has her arms crossed over her chest, eyes darting around the room.

“Maybe I should …” Lena starts, and tears fill her eyes. “Maybe I should leave Seattle.”

“No!” Kara shouts, and she pulls Lena into her. In the moment, she doesn’t think about what the others know or don’t know about them. “If you leave, I’m coming with you.”

She hears Eliza’s sharp intake of breath and realizes belatedly the depth of her statement. And yet, she has no intention of taking it back. She cannot lose Lena again. She would follow her to the end of the world. She was foolish not to make preparations to board the steamer as a miner, and she almost lost her. She won’t let her go again.

“But I don’t want you to go!” Ruby laments and throws herself at Lena. Lena sends Kara a helpless look as she wraps an arm around the girl to comfort her.

“Maybe leaving for a while is a good idea,” Alex says. “How do we know the steamer won’t turn around, or that your brother will turn around once he reaches Skagway? Or send a letter to your mother? You’re not like your family, Lena, I know that now, but they would find you easily. Your maid did.”

Kara doesn’t want Alex’ reasoning to make so much sense. Lena, however, nods in agreement. “I agree with you, Alex.” She thinks for a moment. “I will go to New York. My friend Jack has recently moved his business there. I will be safe with him.”

Ruby gulps, evidently trying to suppress more tears, and nods as if she agrees. She walks over to where her mother and Alex are, and Sam gently strokes her hair. Kara is unsure if Sam’s anger has evaporated, or if Ruby will be scolded later in the day.

Lena turns to Kara. Kara’s heart beats wildly at the sudden proximity of their faces. “I don’t want to take you away from your family, Kara” Lena says softly. “But if they allow it, I would like for you to accompany me. I don’t know for how long I will be away, or if I can ever return.”

Kara wishes she could kiss Lena, but instead she just nods. Lena’s face breaks into a small smile. Their moment, however, is interrupted when both Alex and Eliza call her name. Right. If they allow it. Kara bites her lip and braces herself for the disappointment on both their faces.

Eliza, however, gives her a smile full of warmth and compassion. It’s a sudden reminder of her early days in the Danvers household, when she used to soothe Kara’s night terrors. On the other hand, Alex narrowed eyes can either mean her sister is angry or trying not to cry. Next to her, Lena gulps.

“I will leave you alone with your family for a moment. I need to put my dress back on, this apron is killing me.” She gestures to Ruby, and they hastily leave the room.

“Kara.” Eliza says her name calmly. She smiles again. “You were always destined for things greater than this small shop. And I may not know Lena well, but I’ve heard the way you talk about her, how much you care about her. I think you will be safe with her.”

Kara cannot believe her ears. She swallows thickly. A part of her wants to protest, but she knows deep within herself that her adoptive mother is right. She loves the Danvers and the family they have given her, but she was never meant to be a shoemaker. If her parents were still alive, she would have attended a university out of state. She would have worked a well-respected job and then married a well-educated man. She would not live hand-to-mouth the way they had for the last few years.

“But, Alex …” she whispers, and she cannot hold back a tear from rolling down her cheek. Alex is her rock, her sister given to her not by blood but by fate, and she cannot imagine herself without her.

“Mother is right,” Alex mumbles. Her voice is barely audible, but her words hit Kara with full force nevertheless. “You should go with Lena. It will be safer for both of you.”

Walking towards Kara, she adds: “One condition. You must take off the rubber boots, I haven’t the time to make a new pair before the customer comes to pick them up.” She grins.

Kara laughs through her tears and wraps her arms around her sister. “I’m sorry, Alex” she reiterates. “For simply walking out this morning. For stealing from you. I wasn’t- I wasn’t thinking and-“ she takes in a deep breath.

“You’re forgiven, Kara” Alex reassures her. “Mother is right. You need to go, live your life better than you can here. I will miss you every day, but I will do my best to make life better for us, too. Everything will be so much better when we meet again.”

Kara nods, making herself believe what Alex says is true. They stay like that for a while, until Eliza claps her hands.

“Breakfast. All of us need to eat, it’s not healthy to cry so much on an empty stomach.” She wipes her own eyes with her sleeve, then turns around quickly towards the stove.

“This is my cue to leave, Ruby will be under house arrest until at least Christmas-“ Sam announces, standing up.

Alex hastily lets go of Kara to stop Sam from walking out of the kitchen. “No, Sam. Please stay?”

Sam’s expression softens, and she sighs. “Alright.”

Ruby and Lena choose this moment to come back stumbling into the kitchen. Ruby is giggling, but stops abruptly when she sees her mother. “Mother, Charlie and I need to return the clothing until noon. May we leave now and then return here, to tell Lena goodbye?”

Sam raises an eyebrow. “Return the clothing to whom, Ruby?”

“I cannot say. But I left all my spending money as a deposit, so I would like to give them back as early as possible.” Ruby’s cheeks redden, and she looks anywhere but her mother.

“Then hurry to get your money back, because you will not be receiving any from me in the near future” Sam crosses her arms.

Ruby only nods and gestures for Charlie to follow her. Charlie grimaces and ducks his head, but goes with Ruby. Once they hear the back door fall shut, the women erupt in laughter. Lena clutches Kara’s arm, and Kara isn’t sure what exactly it is that’s so hilarious, but laughter is contagious, and she finds herself gasping for air, unable to stop. Lena’s laugh is clear and pearly, and Kara can’t help but think that if she can be around her every day to hear it, then she’s made the right choice.

When they finally sit down with porridge and tea, their conversation is quiet and calm. Everyone is aware that this is their last time together for what will likely be a long time. Kara tries not to think about it too much. They’re squeezed around the small table, Kara’s arms brushing Lena’s every so often. She turns the teacup in her hands, the one that was once for sale at her parents’ shop, a reminder of her life before this one, a reminder that her life is about to change once more.

Sam’s giggle pulls her out of her wool-gathering. “Oh, look at us. It truly paid off that Lena ripped off the clasp off her new Sunday shoes all this long time ago!”

Kara nearly drops the cup onto the table. She looks up, staring at Sam. “What? What do you mean?”

“Oh, I knew it!” Across from her, Alex laughs. “That tear was most unnatural! I remember it clearly. And when I told Lena that Kara was out running errands, she looked like a kicked puppy.”

Kara glances towards Lena, whose face is bright red. Glaring at Sam, Lena mumbles “I haven’t the slightest idea what you could be talking about.”

It then dawns on Kara, and she wraps herself around Lena. She has nothing clever to retort, so she simply nuzzles her face into the crook of Lena’s neck. She giggles, and Lena’s skin is warm under hers.

They sit in the kitchen long after everyone has finished eating. A sense of urgency is thrumming through Kara’s veins, but at the same time she cannot bring herself to get up from her chair. Too familiar are her surroundings, too familiar the faces of the people she loves. The unknown is waiting for her behind the threshold, and yet she wants to savor this moment just a little bit longer.

Ruby and Charlie return, their stomachs growling almost as much as their chatter, and Eliza makes them breakfast, too.

“You should make sure to make it to Everett by the end of the day, so you can board the next train. You have a long journey ahead.” Alex says when there is a lull in the conversation, and it brings Kara crashing down to reality in an instant.

She swallows. “Right.”

“There is a streetcar that can take us to the Great Northern station in a little over three hours” Lena agrees with a nod.

Kara stares at the scars and stains on the table. When she looks up, Alex is watching her.

“I haven’t even got a suitcase” she mumbles to distract from her inner turmoil.

“Oh, you may have one of mine” Sam offers. “I do not plan on going anywhere soon.” Her gaze flickers to Alex, and Kara thinks she sees Alex blush ever so slightly.

She bites her lip. “Thank you, Sam.”

Lena puts an arm around her, giving her a silent reassurance. Kara’s tension eases, and she closes her eyes. When she opens them again, Alex is smiling at her from across the table.

The goodbyes are long and tearful. The streetcar bell rings to announce the impending departure, and Kara clutches Alex even more tightly.

“Be careful, Kara.”

“I will miss you.”

Lena, Sam and Ruby say their good-byes in a similar manner. Sam hands Lena a small box as a gift. “Thank you” Lena says. “I will write to you, I promise.” She wipes her eyes with her sleeve.

Charlie quietly hugs Kara and then Lena. Kara knows she will miss him, too, but she knows he will learn her tasks at the shop in no time. To her surprise, she sees Alex hug Lena, telling her something that makes Lena chuckle. Lena’s eyes go wide when Eliza shakes her hand good-bye, and it makes Kara wish they’d had more time together here. The streetcar bell rings again, and the sound settles heavy in her chest.

Lena’s gloved hand is gripping hers as they sit down and wave everyone good-bye, the streetcar rumbling under their seats. And just like that, Kara leaves behind everything she’s ever known, except for Lena by her side.

 

***

 

The remaining daylight filters in through the window of their small but clean compartment. Next to the bunk bed there is a wooden table with two chairs and a lowboy in the corner. Kara’s suitcase hits the floor with a thud, and Kara huffs. Lena puts her own next to the wall. The space is barely big enough for two people, but somehow, she doesn’t mind.

She watches as Kara takes in the room, a small smile on her lips. “Would you like the top or bottom bunk?” Kara asks with nonchalance.

In Lena, however, the simple question suddenly makes her chest feel tight. She swallows, then looks at the thin mattresses and the wobbly steel frame. The last time she slept in a bunk bed she was four years old, at an orphanage in Dublin. A top bunk having been the only one left, she would spend the nights afraid to fall out, lying stock-still, staring at the ceiling.

“You can take the top bed,” she croaks out, and Kara grins, oblivious to her inner turmoil, as if that is the answer she had been hoping for. Lena sighs in relief.

Kara takes off her shoes, then climbs up the ladder and plops down on the bed. The frame creeks ominously under her weight. Lena sits down on a chair. They hear a whistle being blown, and then the rumble of the engine makes their wagon move slightly. The train is departing.

Lena thinks about how she started the day on a steamer towards Alaska, and ends it on a train to New York. The ticket seller had been incredulous when they had bought the tickets upon their arrival in Everett, saying most people were headed West these days. Lena had simply nodded. If only he knew.

Above her, Kara hums a melody as the train picks up speed. Everything has happened so fast that it almost feels like a dream, as if she fell asleep in the carriage on the way to the harbor and Lillian might wake her up at the wharf.

Neither of them speaks for a while, and Kara’s humming ebbs off until the only sound comes from the screeching of the wheels. Lena watches the scenery pass by. Slowly, the sun sinks behind the trees and mountains.

“Do you have the box of cookies Sam gave you?” Kara’s voice pulls Lena out of her thoughts.

Lena laughs. “Do you not think it’s time we go to sleep?”

Kara grunts in response, and it elicits another laugh from Lena. They eventually get up and go to the washroom at the back of the wagon. No lights shine through the curtains separating the compartments from the narrow hallway. It seems as if they are truly the only ones heading East. It reassures Lena, somehow. Nobody will see or recognize her for the next two days.

Once her head comes to rest on the worn cotton of the pillow, however, the anxiety makes her chest feel tight once more. With each mile, her old life becomes a memory. She wonders how Lex handled her disappearance, how Lillian is spending her first night alone in the empty mansion. She tries not to think about the shame and disgrace she is bringing to the Luthor name should any of this ever become public.

Gripping the sheets tightly, she croaks out “Kara?”. At first, she thinks Kara might already be asleep, but then she hears the rustling of sheets above her.

“Yes?”

“I’m scared.”

A heartbeat of silence, then the mattress creaks, and in the dim light she can see Kara’s figure climbing down the ladder. Kara wordlessly climbs into her bed, slips under her blanket. Lena scoots over as if they’ve done this dozens of times before.

Kara’s body exudes warmth, and she smells sweet of soap. Her arm reaches over and she pulls Lena closer to her. Lena lets it happen, presses their bodies together, buries her face in the crook of Kara’s neck and sighs contently. Kara murmurs something into Lena’s hair that Lena can’t make out, but before she can think to ask her eyelids begin to feel heavy, drooping with the events of the day. The exhaustion catches up with them as they fall asleep like that.

 

***

 

The next two days pass peacefully. In the mornings, Lena and Kara head to the dining wagon where they are served a simple, but filling breakfast. The passenger wagon is mostly empty, save for a man with a newspaper during the first afternoon. The stops are few and far between, in mostly small settlements. They spend their days in the compartment, talking, reading and playing cards.

Lena is lying in Kara’s lap, head resting on her thigh as Kara sits on the lower bunk bed. Kara is holding a book with one hand, with the other she is caressing Lena’s hair. Lena looks up at her as Kara flips through the pages, re-familiarizing herself with the story before she starts to read.

“’Christmas won’t be Christmas without any presents’, grumbled Jo, lying on the rug”, Kara starts. She alters her voice to match the character’s tone, and it makes Lena smile.

Kara continues the story about the four sisters, and soon enough Lena finds herself completely engaged in it, feeling every emotion Kara portrays as if they were her own. She watches Kara read, watches the way her mouth forms words, and the way her face contorts as she imitates the characters.

When they reach Minneapolis, the sun is about to set. Kara is glued to the window, watching in excitement as the train rumbles over the stone arch bridge and the waterfall comes into view. She turns around and looks at Lena with wide eyes.

“Wow! We really are halfway across the continent, I can hardly believe it!”

Lena smiles at her and grips the strap of her bag a little tighter.

The next train to Chicago is set to depart at 7 o’clock the next morning, the railroad guard informs them, and after buying tickets they find cheap lodging across the street from the station. Kara writes down her name as Kara Zoller, and just like that Lena is reminded that they’re running from her family and that they need to buy a newspaper before they board the train the next morning. She writes down Lena Kieran with shaking hands.

Sleeping next to Kara has quickly become an addiction. Feeling her strong arms wrapped around her torso, her breath tickling her skin, her warmth lulling her to sleep. Tonight, however, sleep doesn’t come quickly. There is shouting in the hallway during the night, and the room smells of tobacco. They get up with the first sunlight.

The number of passengers to Chicago is considerably higher. An infant’s cries fill their wagon as well as the chatter of people. Kara reads the newspaper they bought thoroughly from front to back twice, but there is no mention of the Luthors or their daughter being missing. Lena wonders how fast the news would travel across the continent. Upon their arrival after sundown, Lena is exhausted, and Kara is quiet as well.

The next morning, they learn that the next train to New York is fully booked, and find themselves with almost the entire day to spare before they can board the next one. It fills Lena with dread, wanting to move quickly, always aware of the possibility of being caught or recognized. Kara, however, doesn’t seem to share her worries.

“Lena, what better city to spend a day in! We can walk around and see new streets, new people!” Kara tugs on Lena’s sleeve, excited like a child on Christmas Eve.

Lena cannot deny her, and so they spend the day exploring the city. They ride the L, and Kara marvels at the people and the tall buildings. Lena tries to imagine a younger Sam and Ruby living here, walking on these sidewalks. She buys a postcard, intending to send it to them once they’re safely on the other coast. The memories of friendship found and then given up make her chest feel tight, and she grabs Kara’s arm, holding onto it for the rest of the day.

Their compartment on the train for New York is a little bigger than the previous one, and this time there are more overnight passengers, too. They fall asleep almost as soon as they depart. By now, Lena has gotten used to the screeching of the wheels and the rumble of the engine.

By afternoon the next day, Kara has finished reading Little Women to her, and they spend the rest of the ride in companionable silence, watching the scenery go by until the sun sets again.

Lena wakes with the first sunlight, and as she rubs the sleep from her eyes, she knows it’s not long until Grand Central Depot. An underlying excitement spreads among the passengers as they wake and get ready for the day, nervous glances out of the windows and hushed conversations over breakfast.

Kara’s eyes are round as saucers when they walk along the tracks and towards the exit. Her knuckles are white as she grips her suitcase. Lena cannot help but glance around in awe as well. It’s been a few years since she stepped inside the Depot, and despite its enormous size, it seems to burst at the seams with people and luggage. The smell of steam and tobacco smoke hangs in the air, and the shouts of ticket vendors, newspaper boys and mothers mix.

“What do we do now?” Kara asks.

Lena hums, then searches her purse for one of the letters she keeps there. She holds up the envelope. “We find Jack.”

 

***

 

New York greets them with a blue sky and a light breeze. Lena’s step is determined, and Kara barely has time to look back at the magnificent building the have just exited. It takes up the whole block, its doorways and windows adorned with grand arches. In front of it, men loiter around, and an electric streetcar comes to a halt. Dozens of people spill out and clatter at once.

Across the street, there is a row of shops with awnings at their fronts, looking tiny next to the enormous station building. Restaurant - Park Avenue Oyster House is written in bold letters above the entrance of one of them. Next to them, by a fence, there are several horse-carriages waiting. Coachmen lean onto them, smoking and talking with each other. Without another word, Lena heads in their direction, Kara in tow.

Lena addresses one of the coachmen with confidence, and names their destination. The man looks her up and down, then Kara. Then he nods, squeezes out his cigarette, and helps them load their suitcases onto the carriage. Lena gives Kara an encouraging smile as they climb up, and nudges her shoulder once they sit.

“It’s not far, but you’ll get a good first view of the city.”

Kara only nods and smiles back. Lena’s hand stays on Kara’s shoulder for the duration of the carriage ride, and from the way Lena taps her fingers against Kara’s collar she can tell that she’s nervous, too. The streets are crowded with people, and she watches them as the carriage stops at an intersection. Men and women off all ages and skin colors, their voices blending together and creating a kaleidoscope of sight and sound of the world around her.

Kara has seen Seattle grow from a quiet town to the city it is now, and yet New York exceeds her expectations in a way she can’t quite put into words. She wants the carriage ride to last forever, to see each and every street corner, to take it all in and get lost in it.

When they screech to a halt in front of a big, modern house, she is almost disappointed at their swift arrival. Next to her, Lena takes a deep breath before she climbs down. Lena pays the coachman, and he hands them their suitcases. Once he’s gone, they look up at the house. A staircase connects the sidewalk and the front porch, and the house is surrounded by a small yard. Similar houses line the street, which is not as crowded as the others they’ve passed.

“Let’s hope that Jack is home,” Lena mumbles as they climb the stairs, dragging their suitcases up one step at a time.

Lena pulls on the doorbell, the sound muffled through the door. Quick footsteps follow it, and then the door opens. A small woman, a little older than the two of them, looks at them with a suspicious look.

Lena clears her throat. “We’re here to see Jack Spheer.”

The woman raises an eyebrow. “Name?”

“Lena.” An unimpressed look. “Lena … Kieran.”

“Wait.” The door is being shut in their faces, and they can hear her quick footsteps retreating.

Kara can only give Lena what she hopes is a reassuring look. Neither of them dares move. A minute or so later, the door opens again.

“He is expecting you in his study.”

Lena lets out an audible breath. They enter the house. It’s similar to the Luthor mansion, Kara thinks, if a bit smaller. She wonders if Jack lives alone. Lena had said that he is a bachelor once. Nevertheless, she seems to not have undersold his position as a businessman. The wallpaper looks new, and the floorboards gleam as if polished just for them. They park their suitcases in a corner, then they’re being led up the stairs. The maid, however, turns around as soon as they stand in front of the heavy oak door.

For the second time today, Lena takes a deep breath and knocks.

“Come in!” a male voice sounds, and Lena jumps.

She gives Kara an incredulous look. “It’s really him,” she whispers. Then she opens the door, and Kara follows her inside.

Standing in the middle of the room is a man in his early thirties, a black, trimmed mustache and brown skin. He’s wearing a simple, yet fashionable suit and no hat, clearly not expecting guests. When he sees Lena, he gasps, and Kara watches as they embrace.

“Lena! It’s really you!” He cries. “I can’t believe-“

They separate, and Jack looks at Lena in wonder. Lena grins widely. “What brings you here?”, he asks after a moment.

Lena’s face falls, and she looks back towards Kara. Jack follows her line of sight, and it’s the first time he notices her. Kara gives a shy wave. Jack approaches her, hand outstretched.

“Jack Spheer, head of Spheer Industries. And you are …?”

Kara almost laughs at his sudden formality, a stark contrast to the way he greeted Lena. She shakes his hand. “Kara. I’m Lena’s. Ugh. Friend. We-“

Jack’s eyes wander between Kara and Lena. Kara feels a blush creeping up her neck. She imagines that their secret is safe with Jack, from what she’s heard about him, but it still feels awkward.

“I left my family and had to leave Seattle for a while. I thought we’d be safer in New York.” Lena explains. It doesn’t explain why Kara is with her, but Jack nods earnestly nevertheless.

“Oh, Lena. I’m glad you came here. You can stay as long as you like.” He says it so matter-of-factly, so sincerely, that Kara instantly realizes why Lena speaks of him so highly. It reminds her of Alex.

Jack asks them about their trip, if they have money, what they want to do. They agree to sleep in the guest chamber, and he instructs his maid to help them move the luggage upstairs. The chamber is spacious, the window facing the street.

“I only moved in a few months ago, as I told Lena in my last letter,” he explains.

Kara plops down onto a cushioned chair. She feels exhausted all of a sudden. After days of thin mattresses and small compartments, this chamber feels like a dream. She watches Lena and Jack talk.

“Thank you, Jack” Lena says. “Truly.”

“You’re my friend Lena,” Jack says. “I’m happy to be of help.”

He continues. “Why don’t you two freshen up, take a nap, and we can talk more later? You two look exhausted. Have you eaten?”

“That would be nice,” Lena sighs.

Kara nods from where she’s sitting in the corner, struggling to keep her eyes open. This chair might be the most comfortable one she’s ever sat in.

Jack laughs. He shows them the washroom, once Kara has convinced herself to get up, and when she sees the new, shiny tiles and the plush carpet in front of the water closet and bathtub, her jaw drops. The bathtub seems to be working with gas, something she has only seen in newspaper sales advertisements. All the furniture is adorned with elaborate wood carvings that Kara thinks should belong in a museum instead of a washroom. Lena approves of it with a nod.

Once Jack walks back to his study, Kara gives Lena an incredulous look. “Is this what money can buy?”

Lena laughs, but there’s a sadness behind it that Kara can’t seem to place. She only nods again, then says “you can go first, take as much time as you need. I need to count my money, see how much I have left.”

“I hope I don’t fall asleep in the bathtub, I’m afraid I might drown, it’s so big,” Kara muses. Lena rolls her eyes in jest.

After a long bath with orange-scented soap, Kara falls asleep on the bed, sprawled out with her damp hair on the expensive sheets. Her worn-out cotton nightgown is a stark contrast to her new surroundings.

She wakes up to find Lena softly snoring by her side. Her hair is damp as well, curling beautifully in a way Kara has never seen before. She studies her face, peaceful in sleep, and the freckle on her neck that she wants to kiss again. She scoots closer to Lena, drags an arm lazily around Lena’s torso. Lena mumbles something in her sleep. Kara smiles, then drifts back off.

When she wakes again, the room is illuminated with a harsh electric light, however the outside is already dark. Lena is dressed in a tea gown, combing her hair in front of the looking glass.

“What time is it?” she croaks out.

Lena smiles at her through the reflection. “You woke up just in time for dinner. Jack told us to be downstairs in the dining room by six. Are you hungry?”

As if on cue, Kara’s stomach growls. Both chuckle. Kara sits up in bed, yawning. She gets up and puts on a tea gown as well. She’s never felt strange compared to Lena and her fancier dress, however in this chamber, in this house, in this city, she feels self-conscious. She might have to ask Lena to borrow money to buy something nicer. How she will pay it back, she doesn’t know. She hadn’t thought very far beyond arriving here. She’s not sure if Lena has, too.

“I’ve never seen you in that gown before,” Lena says as if she’d read her mind. She blushes. “It looks cozy.”

Kara looks at the carpet, mumbling a thank you under her breath. They go downstairs towards the dining room, admiring the paintings on the walls. Jack is already seated, and beams at them as they enter the room, gesturing for them to sit down.

“Oh, it’s so nice to have company again!” he says. “My partner has been on business to the District since a fortnight ago. I do find it lonely dining alone.”

Kara has heard that Jack moved to New York to merge his business with his partner, but she is surprised to hear they habitually dine together. She figures that Jack must be lonely in his large house as a bachelor, with only an unfriendly maid to keep him company.

They sit down, and Kara marvels at the fine china and the abundance of food. It’s something she took for granted growing up, and then was so suddenly taken from her when she lost her parents. She had become used to the wooden chairs, the small kitchen, the mis-matched china and simple meals at the Danvers house. It makes her feel guilty. They’re better off now since the gold find, but somehow, now that she has slept off her exhaustion, she finds herself already missing Eliza and Alex.

“Scotch, Kara?” Jack offers, but she politely declines.

Lena however eagerly accepts a tumbler of the honey-colored liquor that the maid hands her. Kara listens to Jack’s and Lena’s conversation as she quietly helps herself to some beefsteak and fried egg. Lena catches Jack up on their journey, about her brother’s questionable decisions, about her father’s sudden passing. Jack is full of sympathy and understanding.

“I’m glad you two came here, truly,” he reiterates and takes a sip of scotch.

They’re being served sweet pineapple for dessert, and Kara does her best not to inhale her portion at once. From the other side of the table, Lena grins at her, knowing well of Kara’s sweet tooth.

“So, what do you plan on doing now, Lena?” Jack asks. “You’re both welcome to stay here as long as you like, but what do you wish to do? Do you have money? Do you wish to find employment?”

Kara nearly chokes on her last piece of pineapple, quickly reaching for her glass of water. Lena is quiet for a moment, fiddling with her hands.

“I have to admit, we didn’t have much time to think. I just needed to get away from Lillian as quickly as possible. I have money and some nuggets of gold I could sell, as well as some jewelry, and my engagement ring.”

She sighs. “I’ve always wanted to attend college, but my father insisted I marry first, because he was afraid I wouldn’t otherwise. Now that he passed, and I escaped from my family, I don’t know where to start. My father insisted I study domestic science and mathematics, to help the family business and be a good wife.”

She bites her lip. “But I’d much rather study chemistry, or engineering! Women study these things. I was focused on coming here, I didn’t think much beyond.”

Jack nods. “What about you, Kara?”

Kara bites her lip. “I thought I could find employment somewhere, I’m good with my hands. Maybe in textiles, or deliver mail. My adoptive family were shoemakers.” She explains towards Jack. She briefly meets Lena’s eyes.

“If my parents were still alive, I would have attended a college and married. They always wanted me to read a lot, to be educated. But now … I’ve been living hand to mouth for a long time. It’s my first time outside of Seattle, really. I don’t know what I can do.” She shrugs, attempting a small smile.

Jack hums and thinks for a moment. He empties his scotch, then calls for his maid to bring him tobacco. His fingers twirl in his mustache. The dining room has fallen silent except for the maid’s footsteps on the floorboards.

“Lena,” he starts. His voice is heavy. “I am aware that this is not a proposal that you may have dreamed of as a girl, or even the one you deserve. But you’ve become a dear friend of mine, and I think we can come to a mutually beneficial agreement.” He glances at Kara, then looks back to Lena.

“I’d like to suggest we marry.”

Lena takes in a sharp breath at the same time that Kara feels all air being pushed out of her own lungs. She narrows her eyes, glancing between Lena and Jack. Jack’s focus is on Lena, seemingly avoiding Kara’s.

Kara likes to think that Lena would reject such a proposal, would tell Jack she has no intention of being married so soon. After all, marriage is what she has just escaped. And she came here with Kara, who she’d shared stolen kisses with, risking being found out. At the same time, however, a memory of her and Lena at the creek comes to her mind, of Lena telling her about London and how she would have liked to marry Jack.

“I think …” Lena says, taking a deep breath. “I find it agreeable and reasonable.”

Kara drops the glass of water she had been clutching tightly. It clatters against her plate and spills over the table cloth. No one bothers to pay it any attention, however.

“Lena …” she whispers.

“Kara …” Lena says, equally breathless. “Please, I still … I-“

Jack drags on his pipe, the smoke evaporating, unlike the tension that hangs dense and thick between them. His glance at Kara is sympathetic, and he clears his throat, coughing lightly.

“I’m sorry, Kara, I know how that must sound to you. I know Lena is your sweetheart-“

Kara gasps. How does he know? She hasn’t kissed Lena, hasn’t even touched her in front of him. Has Lena told him? Has he read her letter? She hasn’t talked to Lena about their shared kisses, about the meaning of the poem she sent her, about Kara’s feelings-

“-and I am aware that Lena does not feel that way about me, or any men.” Jack’s voice comes back to her consciousness. “Just like I am not involved with the fairer sex, though I do find most women I meet to be more intelligent and interesting than men.” He grins.

Kara is dumbfounded, and it takes her a moment to understand. Then, she remembers how Jack dines with his business partner, and how Lena talks about him as her friend.

“Kara, look at me, please” comes Lena’s pleading voice. Kara follows suit. Lena stares at her, wide-eyed, green almost entirely covered by her pupils.

“We can talk more about it later. But Jack tells the truth. It’s mutually beneficial, for you as well. Please, say something?” Her voice shakes, and Kara has to avert her eyes. She hates to see Lena upset, even at her own hand.

She runs a hand through her hair, looks at the table, the dirty plates, the spilled water, the smoke coming from Jack’s pipe. She swallows. She believes Jack, believes Lena. Nevertheless, she wishes it wasn’t so reasonable. She followed Lena across the continent, left behind her family. She hasn’t told Lena yet that she loves her.

Despite the nap, she feels exhausted suddenly. She gets up.

“I wish to be alone for a minute. Lena, find me upstairs later?”

Lena only nods, and Kara waits until she’s out of the room to let her tears fall.

 

***

 

Lena watches silently as Kara leaves the room, the door closing behind her with a thud. Jack gives her a sympathetic look, but doesn’t speak. They’re quiet for a moment.

“I didn’t misspeak, did I?” Jack asks after a while. “When I saw the two of you, I assumed-“

“No, Jack” Lena shakes her head. “You were right. She’s … I wouldn’t have been able to take on the journey without her. She means more to me than she knows. But we aren’t sweethearts, like you and Adam.”

Jack raises an eyebrow, taking a drag on his pipe.

Lena looks at her hands. “We haven’t talked about it. The last few weeks were turbulent, and before that … I was simply enjoying spending time with her. We kissed, and it was wonderful. And now she must think I don’t love her because I agreed to marry you-“ she takes a deep breath, panic rising in her chest.

“It’s not me you should be telling all this to, Lena” Jack says and grins. “Go upstairs and tell it to her. She followed you across the entire continent despite never having traveled before. If that’s not love, then what is?”
